href="/wiki/Wetland" title="Wetland">wetlands</a> are registered under the <a href="/wiki/Ramsar_Convention" title="Ramsar Convention">Ramsar Convention</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-118" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-118">&#91;111&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Politics_and_government">Politics and government</span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Politics">Politics</span></h3>
<table class="wikitable" style="text-align:left; float:right; margin-right:9px; margin-left:2px;">

<tbody><tr>
<td style="text-align:left;"><a href="/wiki/File:Shinzo_Abe_and_Bidhya_Devi_Bhandari_at_the_Enthronement_of_Naruhito_(1)_(cropped).jpg" class="image"><img alt="Shinzo Abe and Bidhya Devi Bhandari at the Enthronement of Naruhito (1) (cropped).jpg" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/15/Shinzo_Abe_and_Bidhya_Devi_Bhandari_at_the_Enthronement_of_Naruhito_%281%29_%28cropped%29.jpg/175px-Shinzo_Abe_and_Bidhya_Devi_Bhandari_at_the_Enthronement_of_Naruhito_%281%29_%28cropped%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="175" height="242" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/15/Shinzo_Abe_and_Bidhya_Devi_Bhandari_at_the_Enthronement_of_Naruhito_%281%29_%28cropped%29.jpg/263px-Shinzo_Abe_and_Bidhya_Devi_Bhandari_at_the_Enthronement_of_Naruhito_%281%29_%28cropped%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/15/Shinzo_Abe_and_Bidhya_Devi_Bhandari_at_the_Enthronement_of_Naruhito_%281%29_%28cropped%29.jpg/350px-Shinzo_Abe_and_Bidhya_Devi_Bhandari_at_the_Enthronement_of_Naruhito_%281%29_%28cropped%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="402" data-file-height="555" /></a></td>
<td style="text-align:left;"><a href="/wiki/File:Prachanda_2009.jpg" class="image"><img alt="Prachanda 2009.jpg" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f4/Prachanda_2009.jpg/175px-Prachanda_2009.jpg" decoding="async" width="175" height="233" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f4/Prachanda_2009.jpg/263px-Prachanda_2009.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f4/Prachanda_2009.jpg/350px-Prachanda_2009.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1047" data-file-height="1394" /></a>
</td></tr>
<tr>
<td style="text-align:center;"><a href="/wiki/Bidya_Devi_Bhandari" title="Bidya Devi Bhandari">Bidya Devi Bhandari</a><br /><small><a href="/wiki/President_of_Nepal" title="President of Nepal">President</a></small>
</td>
<td style="text-align:center;"><a href="/wiki/Pushpa_Kamal_Dahal" title="Pushpa Kamal Dahal">Pushpa Kamal Dahal</a><br /><small><a href="/wiki/Prime_Minister_of_Nepal" title="Prime Minister of Nepal">Prime Minister</a></small>
</td></tr></tbody></table>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Politics_of_Nepal" title="Politics of Nepal">Politics of Nepal</a></div>
<p>Nepal is a parliamentary republic with a multi-party system.<sup id="cite_ref-Al_Jazeera_elections_explained_119-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Al_Jazeera_elections_explained-119">&#91;112&#93;</a></sup> It has three political parties recognised in the federal parliament: <a href="/wiki/Nepal_Communist_Party_(NCP)" class="mw-redirect" title="Nepal Communist Party (NCP)">Nepal Communist Party (NCP)</a>, <a href="/wiki/Nepali_Congress" title="Nepali Congress">Nepali Congress</a> (NC),<sup id="cite_ref-Al_Jazeera_elections_explained_119-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Al_Jazeera_elections_explained-119">&#91;112&#93;</a></sup> and <a href="/wiki/Janata_Samajbadi_Party,_Nepal" class="mw-redirect" title="Janata Samajbadi Party, Nepal">Janata Samajbadi Party, Nepal</a> (JSPN).<sup id="cite_ref-Al_Jazeera_elections_explained_119-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Al_Jazeera_elections_explained-119">&#91;112&#93;</a></sup> Of the two major parties which both officially espouse democratic socialism, NCP is considered leftist while Nepali Congress is considered centrist.<sup id="cite_ref-120" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-120">&#91;113&#93;</a></sup> During most of the brief periods of democratic exercise in the 1950s and the 1990s, Nepali Congress held majority of seats in parliament; <a href="/wiki/Communist_Party_of_Nepal_(Unified_Marxist%E2%80%93Leninist)" title="Communist Party of Nepal (Unified Marxist–Leninist)">CPN (UML)</a> was its competitor in the 1990s.<sup id="cite_ref-Khadka_1993_121-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Khadka_1993-121">&#91;114&#93;</a></sup> After the <a href="/wiki/Communist_Party_of_Nepal_(Maoist_Centre)" title="Communist Party of Nepal (Maoist Centre)">Maoists</a> entered the political process in 2006, they emerged as the third largest party.<sup id="cite_ref-mao_122-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-mao-122">&#91;115&#93;</a></sup> In the aftermath of the <a href="/wiki/2017_Nepalese_legislative_election" class="mw-redirect" title="2017 Nepalese legislative election">2017 elections</a>, the first one according to the new constitution, NCP, formed by the merger of CPN (UML) and CPN (Maoist Centre) has become the ruling party at the federal level and in six out of seven provinces.<sup id="cite_ref-123" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-123">&#91;116&#93;</a></sup> The Madhesi coalition, comprising <a href="/wiki/Samajbadi_Party,_Nepal_(2019)" title="Samajbadi Party, Nepal (2019)">Samajbadi Party, Nepal</a> and <a href="/wiki/Rastriya_Janata_Party_Nepal" title="Rastriya Janata Party Nepal">Rastriya Janata Party, Nepal</a>, which later merged to form JSPN, formed the provincial government in <a href="/wiki/Madhesh_Province" title="Madhesh Province">Madhesh Province</a> (then Province No. 2), though it has negligible presence in the rest of the country.<sup id="cite_ref-124" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-124">&#91;117&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-125" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-125">&#91;118&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-126" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-126">&#91;119&#93;</a></sup> Though Nepali Congress has a significantly reduced representation, it is the only major opposition to the ruling communist party in all levels of government.<sup id="cite_ref-127" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-127">&#91;120&#93;</a></sup> Sher Bahadur Deuba, leader of the Nepali Congress, was appointed as Prime Minister again in July 2021.<sup id="cite_ref-128" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-128">&#91;121&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:172px;"><a href="/wiki/File:BP_Koirala.jpg" class="image"><img alt="Portrait of B. P. Koirala" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b9/BP_Koirala.jpg/170px-BP_Koirala.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="207" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b9/BP_Koirala.jpg/255px-BP_Koirala.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b9/BP_Koirala.jpg/340px-BP_Koirala.jpg 2x" data-file-width="843" data-file-height="1024" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:BP_Koirala.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/B.P._Koirala" class="mw-redirect" title="B.P. Koirala">B.P. Koirala</a> led the 1951 revolution, became the first democratically elected Prime Minister, and after being deposed and imprisoned in 1961, spent the rest of his life fighting for democracy.</div></div></div>
<p>In the 1930s, a vibrant underground political movement arose in the capital, birthing <a href="/wiki/Nepal_Praja_Parishad" title="Nepal Praja Parishad">Nepal Praja Parishad</a> in 1936,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHutt20043-7_129-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHutt20043-7-129">&#91;122&#93;</a></sup> which was dissolved seven years later, following the execution of the <a href="/wiki/Martyrs_of_Nepal" title="Martyrs of Nepal">four great martyrs</a>. Around the same time, Nepalis involved in the <a href="/wiki/Indian_independence_movement" title="Indian independence movement">Indian independence movement</a> started organizing into political parties, leading to the birth of <a href="/wiki/Nepali_Congress" title="Nepali Congress">Nepali Congress</a> and <a href="/wiki/Communist_Party_of_Nepal" title="Communist Party of Nepal">Communist Party of Nepal</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-130" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-130">&#91;123&#93;</a></sup> As communism was trying to find its footing, Nepali Congress was successful in <a href="/wiki/1951_Nepalese_revolution" title="1951 Nepalese revolution">overthrowing the Rana regime</a> in 1951 and enjoyed the overwhelming support of the electorate.<sup id="cite_ref-ram00_131-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ram00-131">&#91;124&#93;</a></sup> In the partyless <a href="/wiki/Panchayat_(Nepal)" title="Panchayat (Nepal)">Panchayat system</a> initiated in 1962 by <a href="/wiki/Mahendra_of_Nepal" title="Mahendra of Nepal">King Mahendra</a>, monarchy loyalists took turns leading the government; political leaders remained underground, exiled or in prison.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHutt20043-7_129-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHutt20043-7-129">&#91;122&#93;</a></sup> A communist insurgency was crushed in its cradle in the 1970s, which led to the eventual coalescence of hitherto scattered communist factions under the <a href="/wiki/United_Left_Front_(Nepal,_1990)" title="United Left Front (Nepal, 1990)">United Left Front</a>.
</p><p>After the <a href="/wiki/1990_Nepalese_revolution" title="1990 Nepalese revolution">joint civil resistance</a> launched by the United Left Front and Nepali Congress overthrew the Panchayat in 1990,<sup id="cite_ref-ram00_131-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ram00-131">&#91;124&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-132" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-132">&#91;125&#93;</a></sup> the Front became <a href="/wiki/CPN_UML" class="mw-redirect" title="CPN UML">CPN (UML)</a>, adopted multi-party democracy, and in the brief period, it was in government, introduced welfare programmes that remain popular.<sup id="cite_ref-Khadka_1993_121-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Khadka_1993-121">&#91;114&#93;</a></sup> After the Maoist Party joined mainstream politics, in the aftermath of the <a href="/wiki/2006_Nepalese_revolution" title="2006 Nepalese revolution">peaceful revolution of 2006</a>, it also adopted multi-party democracy as its official line. The transition period between 2006 and 2015 saw sustained protests from the newly formed ethnocentric nationalist movements, principal among them the <a href="/wiki/Madhes_Movement" title="Madhes Movement">Madhes Movement</a>. RJPN and SPN advocating equal rights and self-governance for the Madhesi people became major political parties in the Terai, <a href="/wiki/Madhesh_Province" title="Madhesh Province">Madhesh Province</a> in particular.<sup id="cite_ref-mao_122-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-mao-122">&#91;115&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-133" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-133">&#91;126&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-134" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-134">&#91;127&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-135" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-135">&#91;128&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Government">Government</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Government_of_Nepal" title="Government of Nepal">Government of Nepal</a> and <a href="/wiki/Constitution_of_Nepal" title="Constitution of Nepal">Constitution of Nepal</a></div>
<p>Nepal is governed according to the <a href="/wiki/Constitution_of_Nepal" title="Constitution of Nepal">Constitution of Nepal</a>. It defines Nepal as having multi-ethnic, multi-lingual, multi-religious, multi-cultural characteristics with common aspirations of people living in diverse geographical regions, and being committed to and united by a bond of allegiance to the national independence, territorial integrity, national interest, and prosperity of Nepal.<sup id="cite_ref-con15_3-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-con15-3">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1096954695/mw-parser-output/.tmulti"/><div class="thumb tmulti tright"><div class="thumbinner multiimageinner" style="width:304px;max-width:304px"><div class="trow"><div class="tsingle" style="width:150px;max-width:150px"><div class="thumbimage"><a href="/wiki/File:Kathmandu-35.JPG" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/36/Kathmandu-35.JPG/148px-Kathmandu-35.JPG" decoding="async" width="148" height="111" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/36/Kathmandu-35.JPG/222px-Kathmandu-35.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/36/Kathmandu-35.JPG/296px-Kathmandu-35.JPG 2x" data-file-width="1984" data-file-height="1488" /></a></div><div class="thumbcaption"><a href="/wiki/Singha_Durbar" title="Singha Durbar">Singha Durbar</a>, the seat of government in Kathmandu</div></div><div class="tsingle" style="width:150px;max-width:150px"><div class="thumbimage"><a href="/wiki/File:Supreme_Court_of_Nepal_01.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/46/Supreme_Court_of_Nepal_01.jpg/148px-Supreme_Court_of_Nepal_01.jpg" decoding="async" width="148" height="111" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/46/Supreme_Court_of_Nepal_01.jpg/222px-Supreme_Court_of_Nepal_01.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/46/Supreme_Court_of_Nepal_01.jpg/296px-Supreme_Court_of_Nepal_01.jpg 2x" data-file-width="5632" data-file-height="4224" /></a></div><div class="thumbcaption">The Supreme Court</div></div></div><div class="trow"><div class="tsingle" style="width:302px;max-width:302px"><div class="thumbimage"><a href="/wiki/File:Nepalese_Constituent_Assembly_Building.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/ff/Nepalese_Constituent_Assembly_Building.jpg/300px-Nepalese_Constituent_Assembly_Building.jpg" decoding="async" width="300" height="134" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/ff/Nepalese_Constituent_Assembly_Building.jpg/450px-Nepalese_Constituent_Assembly_Building.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/ff/Nepalese_Constituent_Assembly_Building.jpg/600px-Nepalese_Constituent_Assembly_Building.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3578" data-file-height="1604" /></a></div><div class="thumbcaption"><a href="/wiki/International_Convention_Centre,_Nepal" title="International Convention Centre, Nepal">Parliament House</a></div></div></div></div></div>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Government_of_Nepal" title="Government of Nepal">Government of Nepal</a> has three branches:<sup id="cite_ref-con15_3-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-con15-3">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<ul><li><a href="/wiki/Executive_(government)" title="Executive (government)">Executive</a>: The form of governance is a multi-party, competitive, federal democratic republican parliamentary system based on plurality. The President appoints the parliamentary party leader of the political party with the majority in the House of Representatives as Prime Minister, who forms the Council of ministers that exercises the executive power.</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Legislature" title="Legislature">Legislature</a>: The Legislature of Nepal, called the Federal Parliament, consists of the House of Representatives and the National Assembly. The House of Representatives consists of 275 members elected through a <a href="/wiki/Mixed_electoral_system" title="Mixed electoral system">mixed electoral system</a> and has a term of five years. The National Assembly, consisting of 59 members elected by provincial electoral colleges, is a permanent house; a third of its members are elected every two years for a six-year term.<sup id="cite_ref-136" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-136">&#91;129&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Judiciary" title="Judiciary">Judiciary</a>: Nepal has a unitary three-tier independent judiciary that comprises the Supreme Court, the highest court in the land, headed by the <a href="/wiki/Chief_Justice_of_Nepal" title="Chief Justice of Nepal">Chief Justice</a>, seven High Courts, one in each province, the highest court at the provincial level, and 77 district courts, one in each district. The municipal councils can convene local judicial bodies to resolve disputes and render non-binding verdicts in cases not involving actionable crime. The actions and proceedings of the local judicial bodies may be guided and countermanded by the district courts.<sup id="cite_ref-con15_3-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-con15-3">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup></li></ul>

<p>Nepal is a federal republic comprising <a href="/wiki/List_of_provinces_of_Nepal" class="mw-redirect" title="List of provinces of Nepal">7 provinces</a>. Each province is composed of 8 to 14 districts. The districts, in turn, comprise local units known as urban and rural municipalities.<sup id="cite_ref-con15_3-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-con15-3">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup> There is a total of 753 local units which includes 6 metropolitan municipalities, 11 sub-metropolitan municipalities and 276 municipalities for a total of 293 urban municipalities, and 460 rural municipalities.<sup id="cite_ref-asia1_137-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-asia1-137">&#91;130&#93;</a></sup> Each local unit is composed of wards. There are 6,743 wards in total.
</p><p>The local governments enjoy executive and legislative as well as limited judicial powers in their local jurisdiction. The provinces have unicameral parliamentary Westminster system of governance. The local and provincial governments exercise some absolute powers and some powers shared with provincial or federal government. The district coordination committee, a committee composed of all elected officials from the local governments in the district, has a very limited role.<sup id="cite_ref-con15_3-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-con15-3">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-asia1_137-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-asia1-137">&#91;130&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Laws_and_law_enforcement">Laws and law enforcement<span class="anchor" id="Crime_and_law_enforcement"></span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Law_enforcement_in_Nepal" title="Law enforcement in Nepal">Law enforcement in Nepal</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:NPP2019_11.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/88/NPP2019_11.jpg/220px-NPP2019_11.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="146" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/88/NPP2019_11.jpg/330px-NPP2019_11.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/88/NPP2019_11.jpg/440px-NPP2019_11.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1280" data-file-height="852" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:NPP2019_11.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Nepal has made progress with regard to minority rights in recent years.</div></div></div>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Constitution_of_Nepal" title="Constitution of Nepal">Constitution of Nepal</a> is the supreme law of the land, and any other laws contradicting it are automatically invalid to the extent of the contradiction.<sup id="cite_ref-138" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-138">&#91;131&#93;</a></sup> The specific legal provisions are codified as Civil Code and Criminal Code, accompanied by Civil Procedure Code and Criminal Procedure Code respectively.<sup id="cite_ref-139" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-139">&#91;132&#93;</a></sup> The Supreme Court is the highest authority in the interpretation of laws and it can direct the parliament to amend or enact new laws as required. Nepali laws are considered generally more progressive compared to other developing countries, and in some instances, many developed ones. The death penalty has been abolished.<sup id="cite_ref-Death_Penalty_140-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Death_Penalty-140">&#91;133&#93;</a></sup> Nepal also has made progress in <a href="/wiki/LGBT_rights_in_Nepal" title="LGBT rights in Nepal">LGBT rights</a> and <a href="/wiki/Gender_equality_in_Nepal" class="mw-redirect" title="Gender equality in Nepal">gender equality</a>. It recognises marital rape and supports abortion rights. Owing to a rise in sex-selective abortion, however, constraints have been introduced. Nepal is a signatory to the <a href="/wiki/Geneva_Convention" class="mw-redirect" title="Geneva Convention">Geneva Convention</a>, <a href="/wiki/Biological_Weapons_Convention" title="Biological Weapons Convention">Conventions/Treaties on the prohibition of Biological</a>, <a href="/wiki/Chemical_Weapons_Convention" title="Chemical Weapons Convention">Chemical</a> and <a href="/wiki/Nuclear_weapons_convention" title="Nuclear weapons convention">Nuclear weapons</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-141" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-141">&#91;134&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/ILO_fundamental_conventions" class="mw-redirect" title="ILO fundamental conventions">International Labour Organization Fundamental Conventions</a>, <a href="/wiki/Treaty_on_the_Non-Proliferation_of_Nuclear_Weapons" title="Treaty on the Non-Proliferation of Nuclear Weapons">Treaty on the Non-Proliferation of Nuclear Weapons</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Paris_climate_accord" class="mw-redirect" title="Paris climate accord">Paris climate accord</a>. Some legal provisions, guided by socio-economic, cultural and religious sensibilities, remain discriminatory. There is gender-based discrimination against foreign nationals married to Nepali citizens.<sup id="cite_ref-143" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-143">&#91;h&#93;</a></sup> Paternal lineage of a person is valued and required in legal documents. Many laws remain unenforced in practice.
</p>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Traffic-controllers_-_Kathmandu,_Nepal_-_panoramio.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/df/Traffic-controllers_-_Kathmandu%2C_Nepal_-_panoramio.jpg/220px-Traffic-controllers_-_Kathmandu%2C_Nepal_-_panoramio.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="161" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/df/Traffic-controllers_-_Kathmandu%2C_Nepal_-_panoramio.jpg/330px-Traffic-controllers_-_Kathmandu%2C_Nepal_-_panoramio.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/df/Traffic-controllers_-_Kathmandu%2C_Nepal_-_panoramio.jpg/440px-Traffic-controllers_-_Kathmandu%2C_Nepal_-_panoramio.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3710" data-file-height="2710" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Traffic-controllers_-_Kathmandu,_Nepal_-_panoramio.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Traffic Police personnel manually direct traffic at the busiest roads and junctions.</div></div></div>
<p><a href="/wiki/Nepal_Police" title="Nepal Police">Nepal Police</a> is the primary law enforcement agency. It is an independent organisation under the command of the <a href="/wiki/Inspector_General_of_Police_(Nepal)" title="Inspector General of Police (Nepal)">Inspector General</a>, who is appointed by and reports to the <a href="/wiki/Ministry_of_Home_Affairs_(Nepal)" title="Ministry of Home Affairs (Nepal)">Ministry of Home Affairs</a>. In addition to maintaining law and order, it is responsible for the management of road traffic, which is undertaken by Nepal Traffic Police. <a href="/wiki/Armed_Police_Force_(Nepal)" class="mw-redirect" title="Armed Police Force (Nepal)">Nepal Armed Police Force</a>, a separate paramilitary police organisation, works in cooperation with Nepal police in routine security matters; it is intended for crowd control, counter-insurgency and anti-terrorism actions, and other internal matters where the use of force may be necessary. The <a href="/wiki/Central_Investigation_Bureau" title="Central Investigation Bureau">Crime Investigation Department</a> of Nepal Police specialises in criminal investigation and forensic analysis.<sup id="cite_ref-Newman2010_144-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Newman2010-144">&#91;136&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-AHRC_145-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-AHRC-145">&#91;137&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-InterpolNepal_146-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-InterpolNepal-146">&#91;138&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Brown2013_147-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Brown2013-147">&#91;139&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-148" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-148">&#91;140&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Commission_for_the_Investigation_of_Abuse_of_Authority" title="Commission for the Investigation of Abuse of Authority">Commission for the Investigation of Abuse of Authority</a> is an independent investigative agency that investigates and prosecutes cases related to corruption, bribery and abuses of authority. At 2.16 per 100,000 in 2016, Nepal's intentional homicide rate is much lower than average; police data indicates a steady increase in the crime rate in recent years.<sup id="cite_ref-149" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-149">&#91;141&#93;</a></sup> Nepal was ranked 76 out of 163 countries in the Global Peace Index (GPI) in 2019.<sup id="cite_ref-150" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-150">&#91;142&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Foreign_relations">Foreign relations</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Foreign_relations_of_Nepal" title="Foreign relations of Nepal">Foreign relations of Nepal</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:172px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Gurkha_Memorial,_Horse_Guards_Avenue_-_geograph.org.uk_-_379881.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/42/Gurkha_Memorial%2C_Horse_Guards_Avenue_-_geograph.org.uk_-_379881.jpg/170px-Gurkha_Memorial%2C_Horse_Guards_Avenue_-_geograph.org.uk_-_379881.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="227" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/42/Gurkha_Memorial%2C_Horse_Guards_Avenue_-_geograph.org.uk_-_379881.jpg/255px-Gurkha_Memorial%2C_Horse_Guards_Avenue_-_geograph.org.uk_-_379881.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/42/Gurkha_Memorial%2C_Horse_Guards_Avenue_-_geograph.org.uk_-_379881.jpg/340px-Gurkha_Memorial%2C_Horse_Guards_Avenue_-_geograph.org.uk_-_379881.jpg 2x" data-file-width="480" data-file-height="640" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Gurkha_Memorial,_Horse_Guards_Avenue_-_geograph.org.uk_-_379881.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Gurkha" title="Gurkha">Gurkha</a> Memorial, London</div></div></div>
<p>Nepal depends on diplomacy for national defence. It maintains a policy of neutrality between its neighbours, has amicable relations with other countries in the region, and has a policy of non-alignment at the global stage. Nepal is a member of <a href="/wiki/SAARC" class="mw-redirect" title="SAARC">SAARC</a>, <a href="/wiki/UN" class="mw-redirect" title="UN">UN</a>, <a href="/wiki/WTO" class="mw-redirect" title="WTO">WTO</a>, <a href="/wiki/BIMSTEC" class="mw-redirect" title="BIMSTEC">BIMSTEC</a> and <a href="/wiki/Asia_Cooperation_Dialogue" title="Asia Cooperation Dialogue">ACD</a>, among others. It has bilateral diplomatic relations with 167 countries and the EU,<sup id="cite_ref-151" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-151">&#91;143&#93;</a></sup> has embassies in 30 countries<sup id="cite_ref-152" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-152">&#91;144&#93;</a></sup> and six consulates,<sup id="cite_ref-153" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-153">&#91;145&#93;</a></sup> while 25 countries maintain embassies in Nepal, and more than 80 others maintain non-residential diplomatic missions.<sup id="cite_ref-154" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-154">&#91;146&#93;</a></sup> Nepal is one of the major contributors to the <a href="/wiki/United_Nations_peacekeeping" title="United Nations peacekeeping">UN peacekeeping</a> missions, having contributed more than 119,000 personnel to 42 missions since 1958.<sup id="cite_ref-155" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-155">&#91;147&#93;</a></sup> Nepali people have a reputation for honesty, loyalty and bravery, which has led to them serving as legendary Gurkha warriors in the Indian and British armies for the last 200 years, with service in both world wars, India-Pakistan wars as well as Afghanistan and Iraq,<sup id="cite_ref-156" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-156">&#91;148&#93;</a></sup> though Nepal was not directly involved in any of those conflicts, and winning the highest military awards, including the <a href="/wiki/Victoria_Cross" title="Victoria Cross">Victoria Cross</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Param_Vir_Chakra" title="Param Vir Chakra">Param Vir Chakra</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-157" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-157">&#91;149&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:172px;"><a href="/wiki/File:DA-ST-96-01245_c1.JPEG" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/68/DA-ST-96-01245_c1.JPEG/170px-DA-ST-96-01245_c1.JPEG" decoding="async" width="170" height="182" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/68/DA-ST-96-01245_c1.JPEG/255px-DA-ST-96-01245_c1.JPEG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/68/DA-ST-96-01245_c1.JPEG/340px-DA-ST-96-01245_c1.JPEG 2x" data-file-width="1364" data-file-height="1461" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:DA-ST-96-01245_c1.JPEG" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Nepal is one of the major contributors to UN peacekeeping missions.</div></div></div>
<p>Nepal pursues a policy of "balanced relations" with the two giant immediate neighbours, India and China;<sup id="cite_ref-158" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-158">&#91;150&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-159" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-159">&#91;151&#93;</a></sup> the <a href="/wiki/1950_Indo-Nepal_Treaty_of_Peace_and_Friendship" class="mw-redirect" title="1950 Indo-Nepal Treaty of Peace and Friendship">1950 Treaty of Peace and Friendship</a> with India provides for a much closer relationship.<sup id="cite_ref-rn50_160-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-rn50-160">&#91;152&#93;</a></sup> Nepal and India share an open border with free movement of people, religious, cultural and marital ties. India is Nepal's largest trading partner, which it depends upon for all of its oil and gas, and a number of essential goods. Nepalis can own property in India, while Indians are free to live and work in Nepal.<sup id="cite_ref-161" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-161">&#91;153&#93;</a></sup> Relations between India and Nepal, though very close, have faced difficulties stemming from <a href="/wiki/Territorial_disputes_between_India_and_Nepal" class="mw-redirect" title="Territorial disputes between India and Nepal">territorial disputes</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-162" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-162">&#91;154&#93;</a></sup> economics, and the problems inherent in big power-small power relations.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBalakrishnan2010196_163-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBalakrishnan2010196-163">&#91;155&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability" title="Wikipedia:Verifiability"><span title="needs inline citation from book source (April 2022)">failed verification</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> Nepal established diplomatic relations with the People's Republic of China on 1 August 1955, and signed the <a href="/wiki/Sino-Nepal_Treaty_of_Peace_and_Friendship" class="mw-redirect" title="Sino-Nepal Treaty of Peace and Friendship">Treaty of Peace and Friendship</a> in 1960; relations since have been based on the <a href="/wiki/Five_Principles_of_Peaceful_Coexistence" title="Five Principles of Peaceful Coexistence">Five Principles of Peaceful Coexistence</a>. Nepal maintains neutrality in conflicts between China and India. It remains firmly committed to the <a href="/wiki/One-China_policy" class="mw-redirect" title="One-China policy">One China Policy</a> and is known to curb anti-China activities from the <a href="/wiki/Tibetan_diaspora" title="Tibetan diaspora">Tibetan refugees</a> in Nepal.<sup id="cite_ref-164" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-164">&#91;156&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-165" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-165">&#91;157&#93;</a></sup> Citizens of both countries can cross the border and travel as far as 30&#160;km without a visa.<sup id="cite_ref-166" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-166">&#91;158&#93;</a></sup> China is viewed favourably in Nepal owing to the absence of any border disputes or serious interference in internal politics, coupled with its assistance in infrastructure development and aid during emergencies; favourability has increased since China helped Nepal during the <a href="/wiki/2015_Nepal_blockade" title="2015 Nepal blockade">2015 economic blockade</a> imposed by India.<sup id="cite_ref-167" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-167">&#91;159&#93;</a></sup> Subsequently, China granted Nepal access to its ports for third-country trade, and Nepal joined China's <a href="/wiki/Belt_and_Road_Initiative" title="Belt and Road Initiative">Belt and Road Initiative</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-168" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-168">&#91;160&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Nepal emphasises greater cooperation in South Asia and actively pushed for the establishment of <a href="/wiki/SAARC" class="mw-redirect" title="SAARC">SAARC</a>, the South Asian Association for Regional Cooperation, the permanent secretariat of which, is hosted in Kathmandu.<sup id="cite_ref-169" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-169">&#91;161&#93;</a></sup> Nepal was one of the first countries to recognise an independent Bangladesh, and the two countries seek to enhance greater cooperation, on trade and water management; seaports in Bangladesh, being closer, are seen as viable alternatives to India's monopoly on Nepal's third-country trade.<sup id="cite_ref-170" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-170">&#91;162&#93;</a></sup> Nepal was the first South Asian country to establish diplomatic relations with Israel, and the countries enjoy a strong relationship;<sup id="cite_ref-171" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-171">&#91;163&#93;</a></sup> it recognises the rights of the Palestinians, having voted in favour of its recognition at the UN and against the recognition of Jerusalem as Israel's capital.<sup id="cite_ref-172" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-172">&#91;164&#93;</a></sup> Countries that Nepal maintains a close relationship with, include the most generous donors and development partners—the United States, the United Kingdom, Denmark, Japan and Norway, among others.<sup id="cite_ref-173" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-173">&#91;165&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Military_and_intelligence">Military and intelligence</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Nepali_Army" title="Nepali Army">Nepali Army</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:172px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Kukri,_karda_%26_chakmak.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/47/Kukri%2C_karda_%26_chakmak.jpg/170px-Kukri%2C_karda_%26_chakmak.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="128" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/47/Kukri%2C_karda_%26_chakmak.jpg/255px-Kukri%2C_karda_%26_chakmak.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/47/Kukri%2C_karda_%26_chakmak.jpg/340px-Kukri%2C_karda_%26_chakmak.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1024" data-file-height="768" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Kukri,_karda_%26_chakmak.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The multipurpose <a href="/wiki/Kukri" title="Kukri">Kukri</a> knife (top) is the signature weapon of the Nepali armed forces, and is used by the Gurkhas, Nepal Army, Police and even security guards.</div></div></div>
<p>The President is the supreme commander of the <a href="/wiki/Nepali_Army" title="Nepali Army">Nepali Army</a>; its routine management is handled by the <a href="/wiki/Ministry_of_Defence_(Nepal)" title="Ministry of Defence (Nepal)">Ministry of Defence</a>. The military expenditure for 2018 was $398.5&#160;million,<sup id="cite_ref-174" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-174">&#91;166&#93;</a></sup> around 1.4% of GDP.<sup id="cite_ref-175" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-175">&#91;167&#93;</a></sup> An almost exclusively ground infantry force, Nepal Army numbers at less than one hundred thousand;<sup id="cite_ref-rag_176-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-rag-176">&#91;168&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-177" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-177">&#91;169&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-178" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-178">&#91;170&#93;</a></sup> recruitment is voluntary.<sup id="cite_ref-179" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-179">&#91;171&#93;</a></sup> It has few aircraft, mainly helicopters, primarily used for transport, patrol, and search and rescue.<sup id="cite_ref-180" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-180">&#91;172&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Directorate_of_Military_Intelligence_(Nepal)" title="Directorate of Military Intelligence (Nepal)">Directorate of Military Intelligence</a> under Nepal Army serves as the military intelligence agency;<sup id="cite_ref-181" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-181">&#91;173&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/National_Investigation_Department_of_Nepal" class="mw-redirect" title="National Investigation Department of Nepal">National Investigation Department</a> tasked with national and international intelligence gathering, is independent.<sup id="cite_ref-rag_176-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-rag-176">&#91;168&#93;</a></sup> Nepal Army is primarily used for routine security of critical assets, an anti-poaching patrol of national parks, counterinsurgency, and search and rescue during natural disasters;<sup id="cite_ref-182" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-182">&#91;174&#93;</a></sup> it also undertakes major construction projects.<sup id="cite_ref-milroad_183-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-milroad-183">&#91;175&#93;</a></sup> There are no discriminatory policies on recruitment into the army, but it is dominated by men from elite Pahari warrior castes.<sup id="cite_ref-184" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-184">&#91;176&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-185" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-185">&#91;177&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Economy">Economy</span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Economy_of_Nepal" title="Economy of Nepal">Economy of Nepal</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:292px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Nepal_Product_Exports_(2019).svg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/26/Nepal_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/290px-Nepal_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png" decoding="async" width="290" height="189" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/26/Nepal_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/435px-Nepal_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/26/Nepal_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/580px-Nepal_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="1100" data-file-height="715" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Nepal_Product_Exports_(2019).svg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>A proportional representation of Nepal exports, 2019</div></div></div>
<p>Nepal is a <a href="/wiki/Developing_country" title="Developing country">developing country</a>, which ranks 165th in the world<sup id="cite_ref-186" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-186">&#91;i&#93;</a></sup> in <a href="/wiki/List_of_countries_by_GDP_(nominal)_per_capita" title="List of countries by GDP (nominal) per capita">nominal GDP per capita</a><sup id="cite_ref-187" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-187">&#91;178&#93;</a></sup> and 162nd<sup id="cite_ref-188" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-188">&#91;j&#93;</a></sup> in <a href="/wiki/List_of_countries_by_GDP_(PPP)_per_capita" title="List of countries by GDP (PPP) per capita">GDP per capita at PPP</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-189" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-189">&#91;179&#93;</a></sup> Nepal's gross domestic product (GDP) for 2019 was $34.186&#160;billion.<sup id="cite_ref-190" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-190">&#91;180&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-191" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-191">&#91;181&#93;</a></sup> With an annual growth rate calculated at 6.6% in 2019,<sup id="cite_ref-192" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-192">&#91;182&#93;</a></sup> and expected 2.89% in 2021,<sup id="cite_ref-193" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-193">&#91;183&#93;</a></sup> Nepal is one of the fastest-growing economies in the world.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title=" (January 2023)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> Nepal has been a member of <a href="/wiki/WTO" class="mw-redirect" title="WTO">WTO</a> since 23 April 2004.<sup id="cite_ref-194" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-194">&#91;184&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The 16.8-million-worker <a href="/wiki/Labour_in_Nepal" title="Labour in Nepal">Nepali labour force</a> is the 37th largest in the world.<sup id="cite_ref-195" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-195">&#91;185&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Primary_sector_of_the_economy" title="Primary sector of the economy">The primary sector</a> makes up 27.59% of GDP, <a href="/wiki/Secondary_sector_of_the_economy" title="Secondary sector of the economy">the secondary sector</a> 14.6%, and <a href="/wiki/Tertiary_sector_of_the_economy" title="Tertiary sector of the economy">the tertiary sector</a> 57.81%.<sup id="cite_ref-National_Accounts_of_Nepal_2018/19_196-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-National_Accounts_of_Nepal_2018/19-196">&#91;186&#93;</a></sup> Nepal's foreign exchange remittances of US$8.1&#160;billion in 2018, the 19th largest in the world and constituting 28.0% of GDP,<sup id="cite_ref-197" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-197">&#91;187&#93;</a></sup> were contributed to its economy by millions of workers primarily in India, the middle east and East Asia, almost all of them unskilled labourers.<sup id="cite_ref-198" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-198">&#91;188&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-199" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-199">&#91;189&#93;</a></sup> Major agricultural products include cereals (barley, maize, millet, paddy and wheat), oilseed, potato, pulses, sugarcane, jute, tobacco, milk and water buffalo meat.<sup id="cite_ref-cia17_200-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cia17-200">&#91;190&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-201" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-201">&#91;191&#93;</a></sup> Major industries include tourism, carpets, textiles, cigarettes, cement, brick, as well as small rice, jute, sugar and oilseed mills.<sup id="cite_ref-cia17_200-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cia17-200">&#91;190&#93;</a></sup> Nepal's international trade greatly expanded in 1951 with the establishment of democracy; liberalisation began in 1985 and picked up pace after 1990. By the fiscal year 2016/17, Nepal's foreign trade amounted Rs 1.06&#160;trillion, a twenty-three folds increase from Rs 45.6&#160;billion in 1990/91. More than 60% of Nepal's trade is with India. Major exports include readymade garment, carpet, pulses, handicrafts, leather, medicinal herbs, and paper products, which account for 90% of the total. Major imports include various finished and semi-finished goods, raw materials, machinery and equipment, chemical fertilisers, electrical and electronic devices, petroleum products, gold, and readymade garments.<sup id="cite_ref-202" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-202">&#91;192&#93;</a></sup> Inflation was at 4.5% in 2019.<sup id="cite_ref-wb19_203-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-wb19-203">&#91;193&#93;</a></sup> Foreign exchange reserves were at US$9.5&#160;billion in July 2019, equivalent to 7.8 months of imports.<sup id="cite_ref-wb19_203-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-wb19-203">&#91;193&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:GPD_per_capita_development_of_Nepal.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b3/GPD_per_capita_development_of_Nepal.jpg/220px-GPD_per_capita_development_of_Nepal.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="137" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b3/GPD_per_capita_development_of_Nepal.jpg/330px-GPD_per_capita_development_of_Nepal.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b3/GPD_per_capita_development_of_Nepal.jpg/440px-GPD_per_capita_development_of_Nepal.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1045" data-file-height="653" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:GPD_per_capita_development_of_Nepal.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Real GPD per capita development of Nepal</div></div></div>
<p>Nepal has made significant progress in poverty reduction bringing the population below the international poverty line (US$1.90 per person per day) from 15% in 2010 to just 9.3% in 2018, although vulnerability remains extremely high, with almost 32% of the population living on between US$1.90 and US$3.20 per person per day.<sup id="cite_ref-wb19_203-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-wb19-203">&#91;193&#93;</a></sup> Nepal has made improvement in sectors like nutrition, child mortality, electricity, improved flooring and assets. Under the current trend, Nepal is expected to eradicate poverty within 20 years.<sup id="cite_ref-204" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-204">&#91;194&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-205" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-205">&#91;195&#93;</a></sup> The agriculture sector is particularly vulnerable as it is highly dependent on the monsoon rains, with just 28% of the arable land being irrigated, As of 2014<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Nepal&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup>.<sup id="cite_ref-206" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-206">&#91;196&#93;</a></sup> Agriculture employs 76% of the workforce, services 18%, and manufacturing and craft-based industry 6%.<sup id="cite_ref-World_Bank:_Nepal-_Country_Overview_2012_207-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-World_Bank:_Nepal-_Country_Overview_2012-207">&#91;197&#93;</a></sup> Private investment, consumption, tourism and agriculture are the principal contributors to economic growth.<sup id="cite_ref-wb19_203-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-wb19-203">&#91;193&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The government's budget is about $13.71&#160;billion (FY 2019/20);<sup id="cite_ref-208" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-208">&#91;198&#93;</a></sup>  expenditure of infrastructure development budget, most of it contributed by foreign aid, usually fails to meet the target.<sup id="cite_ref-209" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-209">&#91;199&#93;</a></sup> The country receives foreign aid from the UK,<sup id="cite_ref-210" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-210">&#91;200&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-211" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-211">&#91;201&#93;</a></sup> India, Japan, the US, the EU, China, Switzerland, and Scandinavian countries. The <a href="/wiki/Nepalese_rupee" title="Nepalese rupee">Nepali rupee</a> has been tied to the <a href="/wiki/Indian_rupee" title="Indian rupee">Indian rupee</a> at an exchange rate of 1.6 for many years. Per capita income is $1,004.<sup id="cite_ref-212" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-212">&#91;202&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Distribution_of_wealth" title="Distribution of wealth">distribution of wealth</a> among the Nepalis is consistent with that in many developed and developing countries: the highest 10% of households control 39.1% of the national wealth and the lowest 10% control only 2.6%. <a href="/wiki/European_Union" title="European Union">European Union</a> (EU) (46.13%), the US (17.4%), and Germany (7.1%) are its main export partners; they mainly buy Nepali ready-made garments (RMG).<sup id="cite_ref-213" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-213">&#91;203&#93;</a></sup> Nepal's import partners include India (47.5%), the United Arab Emirates (11.2%), China (10.7%), Saudi Arabia (4.9%), and Singapore (4%).
</p><p>Besides having landlocked, rugged geography, few tangible natural resources and poor infrastructure, the ineffective post-1950 government and the long-running civil war are also factors in stunting the country's economic growth and development.<sup id="cite_ref-Encarta_214-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Encarta-214">&#91;204&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Development_Failure_215-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Development_Failure-215">&#91;205&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Development-Conflict_Nexus_216-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Development-Conflict_Nexus-216">&#91;206&#93;</a></sup> Debt bondage even involving debtors' children has been a persistent social problem in the western hills and the <a href="/wiki/Terai" title="Terai">Terai</a>, with an estimated 234,600 people or 0.82% of the population considered as enslaved, by The Global Slavery Index in 2016.<sup id="cite_ref-217" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-217">&#91;207&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 2022, Nepal limited import of non-essential goods after its foreign currency reserves dropped. COVID-19 pandemic caused a decline in tourism spending and the money sent home by Nepalis working abroad, which in turn lowered country's foreign currency reserve.<sup id="cite_ref-218" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-218">&#91;208&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Tourism">Tourism</span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:USAID_Measuring_Impact_Conservation_Enterprise_Retrospective_(Nepal;_National_Trust_for_Nature_Conservation)_(26428623908).jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9b/USAID_Measuring_Impact_Conservation_Enterprise_Retrospective_%28Nepal%3B_National_Trust_for_Nature_Conservation%29_%2826428623908%29.jpg/220px-USAID_Measuring_Impact_Conservation_Enterprise_Retrospective_%28Nepal%3B_National_Trust_for_Nature_Conservation%29_%2826428623908%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9b/USAID_Measuring_Impact_Conservation_Enterprise_Retrospective_%28Nepal%3B_National_Trust_for_Nature_Conservation%29_%2826428623908%29.jpg/330px-USAID_Measuring_Impact_Conservation_Enterprise_Retrospective_%28Nepal%3B_National_Trust_for_Nature_Conservation%29_%2826428623908%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9b/USAID_Measuring_Impact_Conservation_Enterprise_Retrospective_%28Nepal%3B_National_Trust_for_Nature_Conservation%29_%2826428623908%29.jpg/440px-USAID_Measuring_Impact_Conservation_Enterprise_Retrospective_%28Nepal%3B_National_Trust_for_Nature_Conservation%29_%2826428623908%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="5760" data-file-height="3840" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:USAID_Measuring_Impact_Conservation_Enterprise_Retrospective_(Nepal;_National_Trust_for_Nature_Conservation)_(26428623908).jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Tourists view a greater one-horned rhinoceros from an elephant in <a href="/wiki/Chitwan_National_Park" title="Chitwan National Park">Chitwan National Park</a>.</div></div></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Tourism_in_Nepal" title="Tourism in Nepal">Tourism in Nepal</a></div>
<p>Tourism is one of the largest and fastest-growing industries in Nepal, employing more than a million people and contributing 7.9% of the total GDP.<sup id="cite_ref-tkptour19_219-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-tkptour19-219">&#91;209&#93;</a></sup> The number of international visitors crossed one million in 2018 for the first time (not counting Indian tourists arriving by land).<sup id="cite_ref-tkptour19_219-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-tkptour19-219">&#91;209&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-220" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-220">&#91;210&#93;</a></sup> Nepal's share of visitors to South Asia is about 6%, and they spend much less on average, with Nepal sharing 1.7% of the earnings.<sup id="cite_ref-thttour_221-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-thttour-221">&#91;211&#93;</a></sup> Premier destinations include Pokhara, the Annapurna trekking circuit and the four UNESCO world heritage sites—Lumbini, Sagarmatha National Park (home to Mount Everest), seven sites in the Kathmandu Valley collectively listed as one, and Chitwan National Park. Most of Nepal's mountaineering earning comes from Mount Everest, which is more accessible from the Nepalese side.
</p><p>Nepal, officially opened to westerners in 1951, became a popular destination at the end of the <a href="/wiki/Hippie_trail" title="Hippie trail">hippie trail</a>, during the 1960s and 1970s. The industry, disrupted by the civil war in the 1990s, has since recovered but faces challenges to growth, owing to a lack of proper facilities for high-end tourism termed the "infrastructure bottleneck", the flag carrier in shambles, and a handful of destinations properly developed and marketed. The home-stay tourism, in which cultural and eco-tourists stay as paying guests in the homes of indigenous people, has seen some success.<sup id="cite_ref-222" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-222">&#91;212&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Foreign_employment">Foreign employment</span></h3>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Child_Labour_in_Brick_Kilns_of_Nepal.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/59/Child_Labour_in_Brick_Kilns_of_Nepal.jpg/220px-Child_Labour_in_Brick_Kilns_of_Nepal.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/59/Child_Labour_in_Brick_Kilns_of_Nepal.jpg/330px-Child_Labour_in_Brick_Kilns_of_Nepal.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/59/Child_Labour_in_Brick_Kilns_of_Nepal.jpg/440px-Child_Labour_in_Brick_Kilns_of_Nepal.jpg 2x" data-file-width="800" data-file-height="600" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Child_Labour_in_Brick_Kilns_of_Nepal.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>While adults are employed in slavery-like conditions abroad, hundreds of thousands of children in the country are employed as <a href="/wiki/Child_labour_in_Nepal" title="Child labour in Nepal">child labour</a> (not including the agricultural sector).</div></div></div>
<p>The rate of unemployment and underemployment exceeds half of the working-age population,<sup id="cite_ref-223" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-223">&#91;213&#93;</a></sup> driving millions to seek employment abroad, mainly in India, the Gulf, and East Asia. Mostly unskilled, uneducated, and indebted to loan sharks, these workers are swindled by the manpower companies and sent to exploitative employers or war-ridden countries under fraudulent contracts.<sup id="cite_ref-224" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-224">&#91;214&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-225" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-225">&#91;215&#93;</a></sup> They have their passports seized, to be returned when the employer grants them leave or terminates their contracts. Most do not get paid minimum wage,<sup id="cite_ref-226" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-226">&#91;216&#93;</a></sup> and many are forced to forfeit all or part of the wages.<sup id="cite_ref-227" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-227">&#91;217&#93;</a></sup> Many Nepalis work in extremely unsafe conditions; an average of two workers die each day.<sup id="cite_ref-228" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-228">&#91;218&#93;</a></sup> Due to restrictions placed on women, many depend on traffickers to get out of the country, and end up victims of violence and abuse.<sup id="cite_ref-229" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-229">&#91;219&#93;</a></sup> Many Nepalese are believed to be working under slavery-like conditions, and Nepal spends billions of rupees rescuing stranded workers, on remuneration to the indebted families of the dead, and in legal costs for those arrested in foreign countries.<sup id="cite_ref-230" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-230">&#91;220&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-ilofe_231-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ilofe-231">&#91;221&#93;</a></sup> Though millions have raised themselves out of poverty, due to a lack of entrepreneurial skills, the remittance is largely spent on real estate and consumption.<sup id="cite_ref-232" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-232">&#91;222&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-ilofe_231-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ilofe-231">&#91;221&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div style="clear:left;"></div>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Infrastructure">Infrastructure</span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Energy">Energy</span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:172px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Kaligandaki_A_HPS_Tailrace_Channel.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/39/Kaligandaki_A_HPS_Tailrace_Channel.jpg/170px-Kaligandaki_A_HPS_Tailrace_Channel.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="255" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/39/Kaligandaki_A_HPS_Tailrace_Channel.jpg/255px-Kaligandaki_A_HPS_Tailrace_Channel.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/39/Kaligandaki_A_HPS_Tailrace_Channel.jpg/340px-Kaligandaki_A_HPS_Tailrace_Channel.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2432" data-file-height="3648" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Kaligandaki_A_HPS_Tailrace_Channel.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Middle Marsyangdi Hydroelectric Dam. Nepal has significant potential to generate <a href="/wiki/Hydropower" title="Hydropower">hydropower</a>, which it plans to export across South Asia.</div></div></div>
<p>The bulk of energy in Nepal comes from biomass (80%) and imported fossil fuels (16%).<sup id="cite_ref-adb17_233-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-adb17-233">&#91;223&#93;</a></sup> Most of the final energy consumption goes to the residential sector (84%) followed by transport (7%) and industry (6%); the transport and industry sectors have been expanding rapidly in recent years.<sup id="cite_ref-adb17_233-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-adb17-233">&#91;223&#93;</a></sup> Except for some lignite deposits, Nepal has no known oil, gas or coal deposits.<sup id="cite_ref-adb17_233-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-adb17-233">&#91;223&#93;</a></sup> All commercial fossil fuels (mainly oil, LPG and coal) are imported, spending 129% of the country's total export revenue.<sup id="cite_ref-234" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-234">&#91;224&#93;</a></sup> Only about 1% of the energy need is fulfilled by electricity.<sup id="cite_ref-adb17_233-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-adb17-233">&#91;223&#93;</a></sup> The perennial nature of Nepali rivers and the steep gradient of the country's topography provide ideal conditions for the development of hydroelectric projects. Estimates put Nepal's economically feasible hydro-power potential at approximately 42,000 MW.<sup id="cite_ref-adb17_233-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-adb17-233">&#91;223&#93;</a></sup> Nepal has been able to exploit only about 1,100 MW. As most of it is generated from run-of-river (ROR) plants, the actual power produced is much lower in the dry winter months when peak demand can reach as high as 1,200 MW, and Nepal needs to import as much as 650 MW from India to meet the demands.<sup id="cite_ref-235" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-235">&#91;225&#93;</a></sup> Major hydro-power projects suffer delays and setbacks.<sup id="cite_ref-236" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-236">&#91;226&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-237" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-237">&#91;227&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-238" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-238">&#91;228&#93;</a></sup> Nepal's electrification rate (76%) is comparable to that of other countries in the region but there is significant disparity between the rural (72%) and urban (97%) areas.<sup id="cite_ref-adb17_233-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-adb17-233">&#91;223&#93;</a></sup>  The position of the power sector remains unsatisfactory because of high tariffs, high system losses, high generation costs, high overheads, over staffing, and lower domestic demand.<sup id="cite_ref-USAID_SARI_239-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-USAID_SARI-239">&#91;229&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Transportation">Transportation</span></h3>
<p>Nepal remains isolated from the world's major land, air and sea transport routes, although, within the country, aviation is in a better state, with 47 airports, 11 of them with paved runways;<sup id="cite_ref-World_Factbook_240-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-World_Factbook-240">&#91;230&#93;</a></sup> flights are frequent and support a sizeable traffic. The hilly and mountainous terrain in the northern two-thirds of the country has made the building of roads and other infrastructure difficult and expensive. As of 2016<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Nepal&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup>, there were just over 11,890&#160;km (7,388&#160;mi) of paved roads, 16,100&#160;km (10,004&#160;mi) of unpaved roads, and just 59&#160;km (37&#160;mi) of railway line in the south.<sup id="cite_ref-World_Factbook_240-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-World_Factbook-240">&#91;230&#93;</a></sup> As of 2018<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Nepal&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup>, all district headquarters (except <a href="/wiki/Simikot" title="Simikot">Simikot</a>) had been connected to the road network.<sup id="cite_ref-milroad_183-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-milroad-183">&#91;175&#93;</a></sup> Most of the rural roads are not operable during the rainy season; even national highways regularly become inoperable.<sup id="cite_ref-241" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-241">&#91;231&#93;</a></sup> Nepal depends almost entirely on assistance from countries like China, India and Japan, for building, maintenance and expansion of the road network. The only practical seaport of entry for goods bound for Kathmandu is <a href="/wiki/Kolkata" title="Kolkata">Kolkata</a> in India. The national carrier, <a href="/wiki/Nepal_Airlines" title="Nepal Airlines">Nepal Airlines</a>, is in poor shape due to mismanagement and corruption, and has been blacklisted by the EU.<sup id="cite_ref-242" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-242">&#91;232&#93;</a></sup> Internally, the poor state of development of the road system makes access to markets, schools, and health clinics a challenge.<sup id="cite_ref-Encarta_214-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Encarta-214">&#91;204&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Communication">Communication</span></h3>
<p>According to the <a href="/wiki/Nepal_Telecommunications_Authority" title="Nepal Telecommunications Authority">Nepal Telecommunication Authority</a> MIS August 2019 report, voice telephony subscription rate was at 2.70% of total population for fixed phones and 138.59% for mobile; 98% of all voice telephony was through mobile phones.<sup id="cite_ref-ntams_243-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ntams-243">&#91;233&#93;</a></sup> Similarly, while an estimated 14.52% had access to fixed broadband, an additional 52.71% were accessing the internet using their mobile data subscriptions; almost 15&#160;million of them with 3G or better.<sup id="cite_ref-ntams_243-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ntams-243">&#91;233&#93;</a></sup> The mobile voice telephony and broadband market was dominated by two telecommunications companies, the state-owned <a href="/wiki/Nepal_Telecom" title="Nepal Telecom">Nepal Telecom</a> (55%) and the private multinational, <a href="/wiki/Ncell" title="Ncell">Ncell</a> (40%).<sup id="cite_ref-ntams_243-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ntams-243">&#91;233&#93;</a></sup> Of the 21% market share enjoyed by fixed broadband, around 25% was again shared by Nepal Telecom, with the rest going to the private Internet Service Providers.<sup id="cite_ref-ntams_243-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ntams-243">&#91;233&#93;</a></sup> Although there is high disparity in penetration rate between the rural and urban areas, mobile service has reached 75 districts of the country covering 90% of land area, and broadband access is expected to reach 90% of the population by 2020.<sup id="cite_ref-World_Factbook_240-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-World_Factbook-240">&#91;230&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Media">Media</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Mass_media_in_Nepal" title="Mass media in Nepal">Mass media in Nepal</a></div>
<p>As of 2019<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Nepal&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup>, the state operates three television stations as well as national and regional radio stations. There are 117 private TV channels and 736 FM radio stations licensed for operation, at least 314 of them, community radio stations.<sup id="cite_ref-World_Factbook_240-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-World_Factbook-240">&#91;230&#93;</a></sup> According to the 2011 census, the percentage of households possessing radio was 50.82%, television 36.45%, cable TV 19.33%, and computer 7.28%.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTE2011_National_Census2,_32_244-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTE2011_National_Census2,_32-244">&#91;234&#93;</a></sup> According to the Press Council Nepal classification, as of 2017<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Nepal&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup> of the 833 publications producing original content, ten national dailies and weeklies are rated A+ class.<sup id="cite_ref-245" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-245">&#91;235&#93;</a></sup> In 2019, <a href="/wiki/Reporters_Without_Borders" title="Reporters Without Borders">Reporters Without Borders</a> ranked Nepal at 106th in the world in terms of press freedom.<sup id="cite_ref-246" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-246">&#91;236&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Demographics">Demographics</span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Demographics_of_Nepal" title="Demographics of Nepal">Demographics of Nepal</a></div>
<p>The citizens of Nepal are known as <a href="/wiki/Nepali_people" class="mw-redirect" title="Nepali people">Nepali</a> or Nepalese. The Nepali are descendants of three major migrations from India, <a href="/wiki/Tibet" title="Tibet">Tibet</a> and North Burma, and the Chinese province of <a href="/wiki/Yunnan" title="Yunnan">Yunnan</a> via <a href="/wiki/Assam" title="Assam">Assam</a>. Among the earliest inhabitants were the <a href="/wiki/Kirat" class="mw-redirect" title="Kirat">Kirat</a> of the eastern region, <a href="/wiki/Newar" class="mw-redirect" title="Newar">Newars</a> of the <a href="/wiki/Kathmandu_Valley" title="Kathmandu Valley">Kathmandu Valley</a>, aboriginal <a href="/wiki/Tharu_people" title="Tharu people">Tharus</a> of the Terai plains and the Khas Pahari people of the far-western hills. Despite the migration of a significant section of the population to the Terai in recent years, the majority of Nepalese still live in the central highlands, and the northern mountains are sparsely populated.
</p><p>Nepal is a multicultural and multiethnic country, home to 125 distinct ethnic groups, speaking 123 different mother tongues and following a number of indigenous and folk religions in addition to Hinduism, Buddhism, Islam and Christianity.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTE2011_National_Census4_5-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTE2011_National_Census4-5">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup> According to the 2011 census, Nepal's population was 26.5&#160;million, almost a threefold increase from nine million in 1950. From 2001 to 2011, the average family size declined from 5.44 to 4.9. The census also noted some 1.9&#160;million absentee people, over a million more than in 2001; most are male labourers employed overseas. This correlated with the drop in sex ratio to 94.2 from 99.8 for 2001.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTE2011_National_Census3_247-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTE2011_National_Census3-247">&#91;237&#93;</a></sup> The annual population growth rate was 1.35% between 2001 and 2011, compared to an average of 2.25% between 1961 and 2001; also attributed to the absentee population.<sup id="cite_ref-248" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-248">&#91;238&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Nepal is one of the ten least urbanised, and the ten fastest urbanizing countries in the world. As of 2014<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Nepal&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup>, an estimated 18.3% of the population lived in urban areas. Urbanisation rate is high in the Terai, doon valleys of the inner Terai and valleys of the middle hills, but low in the high Himalayas. Similarly, the rate is higher in central and eastern Nepal compared to further west.<sup id="cite_ref-249" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-249">&#91;239&#93;</a></sup> The capital, <a href="/wiki/Kathmandu" title="Kathmandu">Kathmandu</a>, nicknamed the "City of temples", is the largest city in the country and the cultural and economic heart. Other large cities in Nepal include <a href="/wiki/Pokhara" title="Pokhara">Pokhara</a>, <a href="/wiki/Biratnagar" title="Biratnagar">Biratnagar</a>, <a href="/wiki/Lalitpur,_Nepal" title="Lalitpur, Nepal">Lalitpur</a>, <a href="/wiki/Bharatpur,_Nepal" title="Bharatpur, Nepal">Bharatpur</a>, <a href="/wiki/Birgunj" title="Birgunj">Birgunj</a>, <a href="/wiki/Dharan,_Nepal" class="mw-redirect" title="Dharan, Nepal">Dharan</a>, <a href="/wiki/Hetauda" title="Hetauda">Hetauda</a> and <a href="/wiki/Nepalgunj" title="Nepalgunj">Nepalgunj</a>. Congestion, pollution and drinking water shortage are some of the major problems facing the rapidly growing cities, most prominently the Kathmandu Valley.
</p>
