<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Citations">Citations</span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Notes">Notes</span></h3>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1011085734">.mw-parser-output .reflist{font-size:90%;margin-bottom:0.5em;list-style-type:decimal}.mw-parser-output .reflist .references{font-size:100%;margin-bottom:0;list-style-type:inherit}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns-2{column-width:30em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns-3{column-width:25em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns{margin-top:0.3em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns ol{margin-top:0}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns li{page-break-inside:avoid;break-inside:avoid-column}.mw-parser-output .reflist-upper-alpha{list-style-type:upper-alpha}.mw-parser-output .reflist-upper-roman{list-style-type:upper-roman}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-alpha{list-style-type:lower-alpha}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-greek{list-style-type:lower-greek}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-roman{list-style-type:lower-roman}</style><div class="reflist reflist-columns references-column-width reflist-lower-alpha" style="column-width: 30em;">
<ol class="references">
<li id="cite_note-18"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-18">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">The entire territory controlled by the monarch seated in Kathmandu at any given time would also be referred to as <i>Nepal</i>. Thus, at times, only the Kathmandu valley was considered <i>Nepal</i> while at other times, <i>Nepal</i> would encompass an area comparable to and largely overlapping with the modern state of Nepal.<sup id="cite_ref-17" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-17">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup></span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-20"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-20">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">The word <i>pala</i> in <a href="/wiki/Pali" title="Pali">Pali</a> language means <i>to protect</i>. Consequently, <i>Nepala</i> translates to <i>protected by Ne</i>.</span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-21"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-21">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text"><i>Nepalamahatmya</i>, of 30 chapters about the Nepal <i>Tirtha</i> (pilgrimage) region, is a regional text that claims to be a part of the <i><a href="/wiki/Skanda_Purana" title="Skanda Purana">Skanda Purana</a></i>, the largest <i><a href="/wiki/Puranas#Mahapuranas" title="Puranas">Mahāpurāṇa</a></i>.</span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-48"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-48">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">On <a href="/wiki/Samudragupta" title="Samudragupta">Samudragupta</a>'s <a href="/wiki/Allahabad_Pillar" title="Allahabad Pillar">Allahabad Pillar</a>, Nepal is mentioned as a border country.</span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-83"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-83">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">This trichotomy is a prominent feature of Nepali discourse and is represented in the <a href="/wiki/Emblem_of_Nepal" title="Emblem of Nepal">Emblem of Nepal</a>, with blue and white peaks signifying <i>Himal</i>, green hills below them signifying <i>Pahad</i> and the yellow strip at the bottom signifying the <i>Terai</i> belt.</span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-92"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-92">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">198 ecological types were first proposed in 1976, which was further revised and reduced to 118, which was further reduced by IUCN to 59 in 1998, which was further reduced to 36 in 2002. As this issue has yet to be settled, the 35-forest-type classification is generally preferred to the ecological categorisation.<sup id="cite_ref-kindl_89-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-kindl-89">&#91;84&#93;</a></sup></span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-105"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-105">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">According to the 2019 IUCN red list, two species of mammals, one bird species and three amphibian species are endemic to Nepal.<sup id="cite_ref-104" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-104">&#91;98&#93;</a></sup></span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-143"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-143">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">However, same-sex marriage with foreign nationals occurring in a jurisdiction that recognises same-sex marriage is now recognised in Nepal, for eligibility to obtain a "non-tourist visa" as dependent of a Nepali citizen, by verdict of the Supreme Court in 2017, as the laws do not make sex-specific distinction in provisions relating to the rights of foreign nationals married to Nepali citizens.<sup id="cite_ref-142" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-142">&#91;135&#93;</a></sup></span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-186"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-186">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">October 2019, IMF update, excludes <a href="/wiki/Somalia" title="Somalia">Somalia</a> and <a href="/wiki/Syria" title="Syria">Syria</a>.</span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-188"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-188">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">October 2019, IMF update; excludes <a href="/wiki/Somalia" title="Somalia">Somalia</a>, <a href="/wiki/Syria" title="Syria">Syria</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Venezuela" title="Venezuela">Venezuela</a>.</span>
</li>
</ol></div>

<li id="cite_note-8"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-8">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">The Sugauli Treaty of 1816 rendered moot the degree of independence of Nepal. The sixth point of the treaty directly questions the degree of independence of Nepal. The fact that any differences between Nepal and Sikkim will be "referred to the arbitration of the East India Company" sees Nepal as a semi-independent or a vassal state or tributary of the British empire.</span>

Then, intermittently, sometime between 60,000 and 80,000 years ago, tiny groups of them began to enter the north-west of the Indian subcontinent. It seems likely that initially, they came by way of the coast. ... it is virtually certain that there were <i>Homo sapiens</i> in the subcontinent 55,000 years ago, even though the earliest fossils that have been found of them date to only about 30,000 years before the present. (page 1)"</span>

</li>
<li id="cite_note-Fisher2018-32"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-Fisher2018_32-0">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1133582631"/><cite id="CITEREFFisher2018" class="citation book cs1">Fisher, Michael H. (2018). <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://books.google.com/books?id=kZVuDwAAQBAJ&amp;pg=PA23"><i>An Environmental History of India: From Earliest Times to the Twenty-First Century</i></a>. <a href="/wiki/Cambridge_University_Press" title="Cambridge University Press">Cambridge University Press</a>. p.&#160;23. <a href="/wiki/ISBN_(identifier)" class="mw-redirect" title="ISBN (identifier)">ISBN</a>&#160;<a href="/wiki/Special:BookSources/978-1-107-11162-2" title="Special:BookSources/978-1-107-11162-2"><bdi>978-1-107-11162-2</bdi></a>.</cite><span title="ctx_ver=Z39.88-2004&amp;rft_val_fmt=info%3Aofi%2Ffmt%3Akev%3Amtx%3Abook&amp;rft.genre=book&amp;rft.btitle=An+Environmental+History+of+India%3A+From+Earliest+Times+to+the+Twenty-First+Century&amp;rft.pages=23&amp;rft.pub=Cambridge+University+Press&amp;rft.date=2018&amp;rft.isbn=978-1-107-11162-2&amp;rft.aulast=Fisher&amp;rft.aufirst=Michael+H.&amp;rft_id=https%3A%2F%2Fbooks.google.com%2Fbooks%3Fid%3DkZVuDwAAQBAJ%26pg%3DPA23&amp;rfr_id=info%3Asid%2Fen.wikipedia.org%3ANepal" class="Z3988"></span> Quote: "Scholars estimate that the first successful expansion of the <i>Homo sapiens</i> range beyond Africa and across the Arabian Peninsula occurred from as early as 80,000 years ago to as late as 40,000 years ago, although there may have been prior unsuccessful emigrations. Some of their descendants extended the human range ever further in each generation, spreading into each habitable land they encountered. One human channel was along with the warm and productive coastal lands of the Persian Gulf and the northern Indian Ocean. Eventually, various bands entered India between 75,000 years ago and 35,000 years ago (page 23)"</span>
</li>
